## **Share Charts** 

Two easy ways to share the charts you have created are to ‘Send Link’ or take a snapshot of the chart image. Send a link allows you to share your entire plotting tool workspace with your colleagues or clients. When the user clicks on a link, Quotient Analytics will open, the Plotting Tool will open, and the charts as you have defined them will plot.  See Send Link icon in Exhibit 21. 

On September 6, 2010, we recommended tactically going long MXN/ZAR (see _EM FX Trade Idea: Buy MXN/ZAR_ , by Vitali Meschoulam and James Lord, September 6, 2010) based on the prolonged underperformance of MXN over the EM FX universe, a possible over-reaction to subpar US growth concerns, and ZAR’s apparent overvaluation. The cross is now approaching multi-year lows and ZAR is relatively cheap. 

Investors can use MSQ to analyze and follow the trade, creating a graph of the cross MXN/ZAR, as we can see on Exhibit 24.

## **Long 10y Korea Bond** 

In the last _GEM Investor_ (see _Global EM Investor_ , by Rashique Rahman and Rogerio Oliveira, September 3, 2010), we recommended investors go long the 10y Korea bond due to its positive carry and rolldown profile and strong demand source from cash-rich onshore pension funds and insurance companies. Also, if Korea is included in the Citi-WGBI index next year as expected, it should benefit by increased offshore demand. 

In summary, the Quotient Analytics platform provides tools that are easy to use and may help identify, monitor and share market insights and trade ideas.

## **Emerging Market Assets and Metrics** 

All metrics are based on close of business prices. Due to the illiquid nature of the products, some metrics may be distorted. 

## **Currency Analytic Metrics: Forwards and Volatility** 

|**Metric**|**Definition**|
|---|---|
|Forward implied rate|The forward implied rate is determined by relative interest rate differentials of the currency pair|
|Adjusted forward implied rate|Forward implied rate adjusted by the implied volatility, e.g., AUDUSD3M Forward Implied|
||Rate/AUDUSDV3M Implied Vol|
|Realized volatility|The annualized standard deviation of the daily returns within a given period|
|Implied/realized volatility|Ratio of implied volatility to realized volatility. Implied volatility is calculated using the Black-Scholes formula,|
||e.g., AUDUSDV3M Implied Vol/AUDUSDV Spot Realized Vol|



## **Rates Analytic Metrics: Swaps and Local Bonds** 

|**Metric**|**Definition**|
|---|---|
|Swap rate|Interest rate swap rate is the fixed interest rate that is exchanged for a floating payment. The floating side of|
||the swap is linked to Libor|
|Swap rolldown and carry|Rolldown and carry comprise the entire PnL in an unchanged environment|
||Rolldown represents the change in the value of a swap as it rolls down the yield curve. For example, one|
||year after entering a 10-year swap, the asset has rolled down to become a 9-year swap. The 1y rolldown|
||on a 10y swap is: 10y Rate – 9y Rate|
||Carry is the value of holding a swap to horizon; it is the difference between the coupon paid (fixed rate)|
||and the floating rate. In basis points running, 1y carry on a 10y swap is equal to: 1y forward 9y rate – 10y|
||rate|
|Spread from spot|Difference between forward swap and spot swap rate, e.g., USS3M30Y-USS30Y|
|Bond yield|Yield-to-maturity of a bond|
|Bond rolldown and carry|Rolldown and carry comprise the entire PnL in an unchanged environment. That is, assuming a bond|
||rolls down the yield curve|
||Rolldown is calculated as the bond’s clean price today minus the bond’s forward price. The forward price|
||is calculated by valuing the bond’s cash flows at a forward date but using today’s discount factors from|
||the bond spline (assuming today’s 3-month discount factor is equal to the 3-month discount factor on|
||some forward date). In calculating the rolldown, the spread of the bond’s price to the spline is kept|
||constant (such that if the bond was rich to the spline, it is assumed to stay rich at a forward date)|
||Carry is calculated as the forward yield of a bond minus its spot yield. The forward yield of a bond is|
||calculated using one of the financing assumptions, depending on the market in question|
||Funding: For AXJ local bonds, carry (in bp running) has been calculated assuming a local currency|
||investor, with funding at the repo rate for the respective country. For CEEMEA, we use the pooled repo|
||rates from the Morgan Stanley Treasury desk|
|Carry Quotient|Divides the level of carry by the appropriately scaled realized volatility of the trade. This allows the user to|
||understand how ‘significant’ the carry type is relative to how much the level of the trade has moved|



**105** 

**M O R G A N  S T A N L E Y  R E S E A R C H** 

## **September 15, 2010 Emerging Markets Analytics** 

## **Sovereign Credit Analytics: External Bonds** 

|**Metric**|**Definition**|
|---|---|
|Yield|Yield to maturity of a bond|
|Z-spread|Difference in the yield on the shifted curve and the bond par curve. Shifted curve is determined from Bond|
||Dirty Price, where the PV of bond cash flows discounted on the shifted curve is equal to the Bond Dirty|
||Price|
|Matched maturity spread|Difference in yield between bond and swap to same maturity|
|Par asset swap spread|Spread applied to floating leg such that swap PV = 100 – Bond Dirty Price|
|Rich/cheap level off the spline|Difference between a theoretical fair value price of a bond calculated off of the Morgan Stanley Treasury|
||spline and the clean price of the bond. The result is converted to basis points running.|
|Yield rolldown & carry|Rolldown and carry comprise the entire PnL in an unchanged environment. That is, assuming a bond|
||rolls down the yield curve|
||Rolldown is calculated as the bond’s clean price today minus the bond’s forward price. The forward price|
||is calculated by valuing the bond’s cash flows at a forward date but using today’s discount factors from|
||the bond spline (assuming today’s 3-month discount factor is equal to the 3-month discount factor on|
||some forward date). In calculating the rolldown, the spread of the bond’s price to the spline is kept|
||constant (such that if the bond was rich to spline, it is assumed to stay rich at a forward date)|
||Carry is calculated as the forward yield of a bond minus its spot yield. The forward yield of a bond is|
||calculated using one of the financing assumptions above, depending on the market in question|
|Swap rolldown & carry|Rolldown and carry comprise the entire PnL in an unchanged environment, for a swap whose maturity is|
||set equal to that of the corresponding bond|
||Rolldown represents the change in the value of a swap as it rolls down the yield curve. For example, one|
||year after entering a 10-year swap, the asset has rolled down to become a 9-year swap. The 1y rolldown|
||on a 10y swap is: 10y rate – 9y rate.|
||Carry is the value of holding a swap to horizon; it is the difference between the coupon paid (fixed rate)|
||and the floating rate. In basis points running, 1y carry on a 10y swap is equal to the 1y forward 9y rate –|
||10y rate|
|Swap spread rolldown & carry|Yield rolldown & carry minus swap rolldown & carry|
|Carry Quotient|Divides the level of carry by the appropriately scaled realized volatility of the trade. This allows the user to|
||understand how ‘significant’ the carry type is relative to how much the level of the trade has moved|



## **Sovereign Credit Analytics: CDS** 

|**Metric**|**Definition**|
|---|---|
|CDS spread|CDS premium rate in bp for buying bond protection for notional amount of $10 million|
|Z-spread|Z-spread of the bond corresponding to the same maturity CDS.  Z-spread is calculated from a basket of|
||bonds and extrapolated to the maturity|
|Basis spread|Difference between CDS spread and Z-spread. Z-spread is calculated from a basket of bonds and|
||extrapolated to the maturity|
|Adjusted basis spread|The difference between CDS spread and the adjusted Z-spread. Adjust the Z-spread for the principal|
||mismatch. This is the difference between a spread metric and CDS as ‘adjusted basis’|



**106** 

**M O R G A N  S T A N L E Y  R E S E A R C H** 

## **September 15, 2010 Emerging Markets Analytics** 

|Modified duration|Equal to the Macaulay Duration divided by (1+ (bond yield/k)) where k is the number of compounding<br>periods per year<br>Macaulay Duration = ∑(cash flow discounted with ytm X time to cash flow)/price of the bond|
|---|---|



## **Corporate Analytics** 

|**Metric**|**Definition**|
|---|---|
|Yield|Yield to maturity of a bond|
|Price|Market price of a bond|
|OAS|A spread is added to the discount curve to equate present value with market value prices. This spread is|
||known as OAS, which represents the average spread over the Libor (discount) curve that will be earned on|
||the security over the remaining life|
|Duration|Duration, or Macaulay duration, is the PV-weighted time to receive each cash flow|
||Macaulay Duration = ∑(cash flow discounted with ytm X time to cash flow)/price of the bond|



## **Central Bank Expectations** 

|**Country**|**Forward Rate Assumptions**|
|---|---|
|Chile|Estimated forward bank rate = spot bank rate + swap forward rate − Libor rate|
||Libor rate: CHIBNOM index rate|
||Swap curve forward period: 3M|
|Brazil, Mexico|Implied Forwards based on Futures|
|Czech, Hungary, Israel, Poland, South|Estimates of the spread between the forward starting agreements (FRAs) and spot 3m interbank fixings|
|Africa||
|China, India, Korea, Malaysia, Taiwan,|Estimates of the spread between the forward starting and spot 3m interest rate swaps|
|Thailand||

Equal-weight (E or Equal) - The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the total return of the relevant country MSCI Index or the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis over the next 12-18 months. Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
